Preface

Lao She (1899～1966), whose original name was Shu Qingchun, also named Sheyu, was a native of Beijing. He lost his father when he was young and came from a poor family. He was influenced by the social life, ideological concepts and aesthetic tastes of the lower class civilians in old Beijing. In 1924, he went to England to teach and began literary creation, attracting attention with his humorous style. After returning to China in 1930, he created works such as "Camel Xiangzi" and "Divorce", establishing his status as the first urban civilian literature in the history of modern literature. During the Anti-Japanese War, he presided over the work of the All-China Literary and Art Circles Anti-Enemy Association and wrote the million-word novel "Four Generations Under One Roof". After the founding of New China, he produced dramas such as "Longxugou" and "Teahouse" and won the title of "People's Artist".

The novel tells the tragic story of a rickshaw driver named Xiangzi in Peking in the old days.

Xiangzi moved from the countryside to the city and became a rickshaw puller. He is young and strong, has no bad habits, and is full of confidence in the future. His only wish is to buy a car of his own and become an "independent" and "free" driver. However, the three setbacks in buying a car have shattered his life ideals and plunged him into despair.

Emotionally, Xiangzi also lost his initiative. Huniu, the daughter of the owner of the garage Liu Siye, liked Xiangzi. Although Xiangzi hated her for being old and ugly, he did not resist her temptation and married her. After the marriage, Xiangzi used Huniu's private money to buy an old car owned by Erqiangzi. But not long after, Huniu died of dystocia, and Xiangzi had to sell the car to bury her. This time the family was broken up and everyone died, making Xiangzi extremely sad. After Huniu died, Xiao Fuzi's careful care made him truly fall in love with this poor girl. He seemed to have the courage to live again, but fate still liked to play tricks on him. Due to the pressure of life, Xiao Fuzi became a prostitute, and hung himself before Xiangzi could "get ready". This made Xiangzi's last glimmer of hope come to nothing.

From then on, Xiangzi completely collapsed and fell. He ate, drank, whored, gambled, cheated and cheated, and even sold his life, until he finally became a dregs of society and a walking corpse, waiting for death.

"Luotuo Xiangzi" uses Beijing spoken language, dialects, and some descriptions of old Beijing's customs and customs. It reads in a friendly, natural and catchy way. It is a classic work of modern vernacular novels.

one

What we want to talk about is Xiangzi, not Camel. "Camel" is just a nickname for Xiangzi. So, let’s talk about Xiangzi first. As for the relationship between "Camel" and Xiangzi, let's just talk about it casually here.

There are many schools of foreign rickshaw pullers in Peiping: those who are young, strong, and agile, who pay attention to renting beautiful cars, pulling them "all day long", and being free to put the cart out and put it away whenever they like; those who pull out the cart at a fixed "port"[1]

Or once the house door is opened, waiting for the owner to take the express train; if it is done, maybe one or two yuan can be obtained at once; if it happens, the whole day may be wasted, and even the "car share" is not secured, but it doesn't matter. Guys in this group probably have two hopes: either to get a rental car; or to buy their own car. Once you have your own car, it doesn't matter if you get a rental car or rent a car. Anyway, the car is your own.

Those who are a little older than this group, or those who are a little less able to run due to physical reasons, or those who dare not waste a day due to family reasons, probably most of them pull 80% new cars; both the people and the cars are quite beautiful, so they can still maintain considerable dignity when asking for prices. The driver of this group may pull for "a whole day" or "for half a day". In the latter case, because they still have considerable energy, they are always "late" no matter winter or summer[2]

. Of course, you need more attention and skills at night than during the day; you will naturally earn more money.

Those who are over forty and under twenty may find it difficult to gain a position in the first two factions. Their cars were broken and they didn't dare to stay late, so they could only get out early in the morning, hoping to move from early morning to three or four in the afternoon, so they could get out of the car and eat their own grain. Their cars are broken and they run slowly, so they walk more and ask for less money. They are the ones who go to the melon market, fruit market, and vegetable market to carry goods. They have little money, but there is no need to run fast.

Here, those under the age of twenty—some have been doing this since they were eleven or twelve years old—few can transform into beautiful coachmen after the age of twenty, because they were injured in childhood and it is difficult to become strong. They may have been pulling rickshaws all their lives, but they have never been in the limelight even pulling rickshaws. Some of the people over forty have been pulling carts for ten or eight years, and the deterioration of their muscles has made them sit behind others. They gradually know that sooner or later they will die on the road. Their manner of pulling a cart, their adaptability when bargaining, and their walking around and around are all enough to remind them of their past glory, and to encourage those who come after them with their noses. However, this glory cannot reduce the darkness of the future at all, and they themselves often sigh slightly when wiping their sweat. However, compared with other rickshaw drivers in their forties, they seem to have not yet reached home. These people had never thought that they could have anything to do with a foreign car before, and they only picked up the handlebars when the line between life and death was no longer clear. The patrolman or school janitor who was dismissed, the hawker who had exhausted all his capital, or the unemployed craftsman, who had nothing to sell and no job left, gritted his teeth and with tears in his eyes, embarked on this road to death. These people have sold their prime period of life, and now they are pouring their blood and sweat on the road. If you have no strength, no experience, and no friends, you will not get good cheer even among your peers. They pull the poorest carts, and the belts may be deflated many times a day; while pulling people, they have to beg for forgiveness, even though fifteen copper coins is considered a sweet deal.

In addition, due to the unique environment and knowledge, some cart drivers formed another sect. Those who were born in Xiyuan and Haidian are naturally more convenient to go to Xishan, Yanjing, and Tsinghua; similarly, those who are outside Andingmen can go to Qinghe, Beiyuan; those who are outside Yongdingmen can go to Nanyuan... This is a long trip, and you don't want to take a single seat; because one trip is just one trip, and you don't bother to scrape together for three or five coppers. But they are not as spirited as the rickshaw pullers in Dongjiaomin Lane. These fastidious foreign traders pull them all the way from Jiaomin Lane to Yuquan Mountain, the Summer Palace or Xishan Mountain. Being angry is not a big deal. Most of the reasons why ordinary coachmen cannot compete for this business are because these people who eat foreign food have some unique knowledge and they can speak foreign languages. The British soldiers and the French soldiers all knew about Longevity Mountain, Yonghe Temple, and the "Eight Hutongs". They have their own foreign language and do not teach it to others. Their running style is also special. They take four or six steps, neither fast nor slow. They keep their heads down and don't look away. They walk close to the roadside, giving off an air of being indifferent to the world and having expertise of their own. Because they are holding foreigners, they don't need to wear a hoodie, but they all wear long-sleeved white coats, white or black trousers with particularly fat legs, and thin straps on their ankles; their feet are wide, double-faced, blue cloth shoes with thousand-layer soles; they are clean, neat, and elegant. Once they see such clothes, other drivers will no longer come to compete for seats and race cars. They seem to belong to another industry.

With this simple analysis, when we talk about Xiangzi's status, it is as accurate as talking about - we hope - a certain kind of nail on a machine. Before Xiangzi became associated with the nickname "Camel", he was a relatively free foreign rickshaw driver. This means that he belongs to the category of young, strong and owning his own car: his own car and his own life are all in his own hands, a high-class rickshaw driver. This is no easy task. It took one, two, or at least three or four years; one drop of sweat, two drops of sweat, or countless thousands of drops of sweat to earn that car. I earned that car through gritting my teeth in the wind and rain, and suffering from food and tea. That car was the total result and reward of all his struggles and hardships, like a badge of a warrior who had experienced many battles. When he was renting someone else's car, he would go from morning to night, from east to west, from south to north, like a top being spun by others; he had no self. But in this rotation, his eyes were not dazzled, and his mind was not confused. He kept thinking about a car far away, a car that could make him free and independent, like his own hands and feet. With his own car, he can no longer be annoyed by the people who tie the car, and he does not have to deal with others; with his own strength and a foreign car, he can have food when he opens his eyes.

He is not afraid of hardship, and he does not have the bad habits of ordinary rickshaw drivers that are forgivable and inconvenient to follow. His intelligence and hard work are enough to make his aspirations a reality. If his environment was better, or he received more education, he would definitely not fall into the "rubber group", and no matter what he did, he would never let down his opportunity. Unfortunately, he had to pull a rickshaw; well, in this job he also proved his ability and intelligence. It was as if he could be a good ghost even in hell. He grew up in the countryside, lost his parents and several acres of thin farmland, and ran to the city when he was eighteen years old. With the strength and honesty of a country boy, he has done almost everything where he can make a living by working hard. However, he soon realized that pulling carts was an easier way to make money; doing other hard work, the income was limited; pulling carts had many changes and opportunities, and he didn't know when and where he would encounter rewards that were more than expected. Of course, he also knew that such an opportunity was not entirely accidental, but that both the person and the car must be in good spirits and have goods to sell in order to meet someone who knows the goods. After thinking about it, he believed that he had the qualifications: he was strong and young; the only difference was that he had not run yet and did not dare to pull a beautiful car as soon as he got started. But this is not an insurmountable difficulty. With his body and strength as a basis, he only needs to test it for ten days and a half, and he will definitely be able to run well. Then he can rent a new car, and maybe he can get a chartered car soon. After living frugally for a year or two, or even three or four years, he will be able to buy a car by himself, a beautiful car! Looking at his powerful muscles, he thought that it was only a matter of time. This was a wish and goal that could be achieved, and it was definitely not a dream!

His stature and muscles have developed in advance of his years; in his twenties, he is already very large and tall. Although his limbs have not been molded into a certain pattern by the years, he already looks like an adult - an adult with an innocent and mischievous look on his face and body. Looking at the high-class coachman, he planned how to get into his waist so as to show off his iron fan-like chest and straight back; he turned his head to look at his own shoulders, how wide and majestic they were! After cutting off the waist, he put on white trousers with fat legs, and tied the trousers with chicken intestine straps, revealing those "outstanding" big feet! Yes, he would undoubtedly be the best driver; he laughed to himself like a fool. He had no appearance; what made him lovely was the spirit in his face. His head is not very big, with round eyes, a fleshy nose, two very short and thick eyebrows, and his head is always shaved brightly. There is no extra flesh on the cheeks, and the neck is almost as thick as the side of the head; the face is always flushed, and what is particularly bright is a large scar between the cheekbone and the right ear - when I was a child, I slept under a tree.

Got chewed by a donkey. He didn't pay much attention to his appearance. He loved his face as much as he loved his body, both of which were so strong and hard; he seemed to count his face among his limbs, as long as they were hard and hard. Yes, after arriving in the city, he could still stand upside down for a long time with his head down. Standing like this, he felt that he was like a tree, with no part up and down that was not straight.

He is indeed a bit like a tree, strong, silent, and alive. He has his own plans and some ideas, but he doesn't feel comfortable telling others about them. Among foreign rickshaw pullers, personal grievances and difficulties are the public talk. At the "rickshaw gate", in small teahouses, and in large courtyards, everyone reports, describes or makes noises about their own affairs, and then these things become everyone's property, passed from place to place like folk songs. Xiangzi was from the country, and he could not speak as eloquently as people from the city. If eloquence was a gift, he was born not to talk much, and he had no desire to imitate the sharp-tongued people in the city. He knows his stuff and doesn't like to discuss it with others. Because his mouth is always idle, he has time to think, and his eyes seem to be looking into his own heart. As long as he makes up his mind, he will follow the path opened in his heart; if it doesn't work, he can stay silent for a day or two, gritting his teeth as if biting his own heart! He decided to pull the cart and did so. After renting a broken car, he practiced his legs first. I didn’t get any money on the first day. Business was good the next day, but after lying down for two days, his ankles were swollen like two gourds and he could no longer lift them up. He endured the pain no matter what. He knew that this was inevitable and that this was a hurdle that must be passed when pulling a cart. Unless he passed this level, he could not run boldly.

After his feet healed, he dared to run. This made him very happy, because there was nothing else to be afraid of: he was very familiar with the place names, and it didn't matter even if he sometimes took a detour. Fortunately, he had plenty of strength. The method of pulling a cart is not too difficult to understand based on his experience of pushing, pulling, carrying, and picking. Besides, he has his own ideas: pay more attention and strive less for victory, and nothing will go wrong. As for bargaining for seats, he is too slow-talking and aggressive to get past those old guys. Knowing this shortcoming, he simply didn't go to the "car entrance"; he put it wherever there was no car. In this secluded place, he could negotiate the price calmly, and sometimes he would not ask for the price, and would just say: "Sit on it, and we'll give it to you!" His look was so honest, and his face was so simple and cute, people seemed to have no choice but to trust him, and didn't dare to think that this stupid big man could knock people off. Even if people are suspicious, they can only suspect that he is a country boy who has recently come to the city. He probably doesn't know the way, so he can't tell the price. When people ask, "Do you know him?" he pretends to be stupid or smiles in a playful way, leaving people wondering what to do.

Within two or three weeks he had slipped his leg out. He knows his running style looks good. Running style is evidence of the driver's ability and qualifications. The one with his legs splayed on the ground like a pair of cattail fans was undoubtedly a newcomer who had just come from the countryside. The ones with their heads bowed very deep, their feet scraping the ground, running and walking at about the same speed, but showing signs of running, were those over fifty years old. Those who are experienced but have little strength have another method: they draw their chests inward and have a deep angle; they lift their legs very high; and they poke their heads as they walk. In this way, they give the appearance of running hard, but in fact they are not faster than others at all; they rely on their "appearance" to maintain their dignity. Of course, Xiangzi would never adopt these postures. His legs are long and his stride is long, and his waist is very stable. He runs without much noise, and stretches a little with every step. The handlebars don't move, making the seat feel safe and comfortable. When he said stop, no matter how fast he was running, he would rub his big feet lightly on the ground and then he would stop; his strength seemed to be able to reach every part of the car. His back was slightly bent and his hands were loosely clasped around the handlebars. He moved neatly and accurately; he ran very fast without any hint of haste, fast and without danger. Even in a chartered car, it must be considered very expensive.

He got a new car. From the day he changed the car, he found out that the one he rented - with a soft bow, copper work, large raincloth curtains, double lights, and a large copper horn with a thin neck - was worth more than a hundred yuan; if the paint and copper work had a little iron, a hundred yuan would be enough to stop it. Roughly speaking, as long as he has 100 yuan, he can buy a car. Suddenly I thought, if I can have a dime left a day, one hundred yuan is a thousand days, a thousand days! Putting a thousand days together, he could hardly calculate how far it was. However, he made up his mind to buy a car in a thousand days or ten thousand days! The first step he should take, he thought about it, is to get a chartered car. If he meets a man who socializes a lot and has many dinner parties, on average there are more than ten dinner parties in a month, he can save two or three yuan for transportation and meals. In addition, he saves another eighty yuan every month, maybe three to five yuan, and he can have fifty or sixty yuan left in a year! In this way, his hope will be much closer. He doesn't smoke, drink, or gamble. He doesn't have any hobbies or family burdens. As long as he grits his teeth, everything will be fine. He swore to himself that in a year and a half, he - Xiangzi - must make his own car! It's a current one, don't see an old car like a new one.

He really got a monthly subscription. However, the facts do not entirely help hope. Yes, he gritted his teeth, but a year and a half later he still hadn't fulfilled his wish. The chartered car was indeed pulled up, and things were carefully watched; unfortunately, things in the world are not one-dimensional. He takes care of himself, but his employer will not let him go because of this; it may be three or two months, or ten or eight days, blow it! He had to find something else to do. Naturally, he had to look for work while also looking for a place to sit; he couldn't be idle while riding and looking for horses. At this time of year, he often made mistakes. He still had to work hard, not just for a day of chewing grains[3]

, and continue to save money to buy a car. But it's never appropriate to force yourself up: when he pulled up the car, he couldn't concentrate on running. He seemed to be thinking about something. The more he thought about it, the more frightened and angry he became. If this continues, when will I be able to buy a car? Why is this so? Could it be that I am not a strong person? While he was thinking so wildly, he forgot his usual caution. Broken copper and magnets were stuck on the leather wheels, and the cannon was fired; the car had to be put away. More seriously, I sometimes bumped into pedestrians, and once I even lost my axle cover in my rush to squeeze past. If he was pulling a chartered car, these mistakes would never have happened; as soon as he put things aside, he felt unhappy and became a little stunned. If he crashes his car, he will naturally have to pay for it; this makes him even more anxious and adds fuel to the fire; for fear of causing even greater trouble, he sometimes sleeps all day long. When he opened his eyes, he realized that the day had passed in vain, and he regretted and hated himself again. Also, in this period, the more anxious he is, the more he suffers from himself, and the more irregular he eats and drinks. He thinks that he is made of iron, but he can still get sick if he dares to do so. When he was sick, he was reluctant to spend money to buy medicine, so he held on. As a result, his illness became more and more serious, and he not only had to buy medicine, but also had to rest for several days at a stretch. These difficulties made him grit his teeth and work harder, but the money to buy a car did not speed up the process. In three years, he collected enough money!

He couldn't wait any longer. The original plan was to buy the most complete, latest and most desirable car, but now I have to settle for a hundred dollars. Can't wait any longer; if something happens, I'll lose a few more! It happened that there was a car that had just been ordered (custom-made and he had no money to pick it up) that was pretty much the same as what he expected. It was originally worth more than a hundred, but because he gave up the deposit, the car shop was willing to ask for less. Xiangzi's face was red, his hands were trembling, and he slapped out ninety-six yuan: "I want this car!" The shop owner planned to squeeze in a round number, and after talking for a long time, he pulled his car out and in again, opened the shed, put it down again, and honked the horn. If one of the steel bars is soft, take it back and throw it in my face! One hundred yuan, let's blow it!" Xiangzi counted the money again: "I want this car, ninety-six!" The shop owner knew that he had met a thoughtful person. He looked at the money and sighed: "Become a friend, and the car is yours; guarantee for six months: Unless you smash the big box, I will repair it for free; take the insurance policy!"

Xiangzi's hands trembled even more. He picked up the insurance policy and pulled up the car, almost crying. Pull it to a secluded place, look at your car carefully, and try to shine your face on the paint board! The more I look at it, the cuter it becomes, and even the things that don’t quite meet my ideals can be forgiven, because it’s already my own car. It seemed that he could take a break from looking at the car for the time being. He sat on the new footrest of the water dustpan and looked at the shiny brass horn on the handlebar. He suddenly remembered that he was twenty-two years old this year. Because his parents died early, he forgot when his birthday was. Since coming to the city, he has not celebrated a birthday. Well, I bought a new car today. Even if it’s my birthday, it’s easy to remember that what’s yours belongs to the car, and since the car is your own hard work, there’s no reason why you can’t count people and cars together.

How to live this "double life"? Xiangzi had an idea: the first transaction must be with a well-dressed person, definitely not a woman. It is best to pull to the front door, followed by Dongan Market. When he arrived, he should have a meal at the best food stall, such as hot biscuits stuffed with fried mutton or something like that. After eating, if there is a good deal, I will buy one or two more; if there is not, I will collect the car; it is my birthday!

Since he got this car, his life has become more and more exciting. Whether it's a monthly contract or a free contract, he doesn't have to worry about his "car share" every day. How much money he gets is all his own. When you feel comfortable in your heart, you will be more kind to others, and your business will go more smoothly. After working for half a year, his hope became even greater: if he continues like this, if he works for two years, or at most two years, he will be able to buy another car, one, two... and he can also drive a factory!

However, most hopes are in vain, and Xiangzi's is no exception.

[1]

The car entrance is the parking place.

[2]

Staying late means leaving the car after four o'clock in the afternoon and waiting until dawn.

[3]

Chew the grain and eat it.

two

Since buying a car, Xiangzi has run faster. The work at hand became easier, and I became more courageous. But he was always extra careful about his car, but when he looked at himself and then at his car, he felt something was wrong, if he didn't run quickly.

Since Xiangzi came to the city, he has grown more than an inch taller. He himself felt that he still had to grow. Yes, his skin and appearance were firmer and firmer, and he had a small mustache on his upper lip; but he thought he should grow taller. When he came to a small house door or a street door and had to lower his head to get in, he didn't say anything, but he secretly liked it because he was already so tall and felt that he was still growing. He seemed to be both an adult and a child, which was very interesting.

Such a big man pulls into such a beautiful car, his own car, the bow is so soft that even the handlebars move slightly; the cabin is so bright, the cushions are so white, and the horn is so loud; how can he be worthy of himself if he can't run fast, and how can he be worthy of that car? This is not vanity, but seems to be a responsibility. Unless you run fast or fly, you will not be able to give full play to your own power and the beauty of the car. That car is really cute. After driving it for half a year, it seems that there is consciousness and emotion everywhere. Whenever Xiangzi twists his waist, squats down, or straightens his back, the car immediately responds and provides Xiangzi with the most satisfactory help. There is no awkwardness between him and it. When he arrived at a place with few people on the ground, Xiangzi could hold the handle with one hand, and the slightly squeaking leather wheel urged him to run quickly and steadily like a sharp breeze. When they got to the spot, Xiangzi's clothes were sweating so hard that they looked like they had just been fished out of a basin. He felt tired, but it was very pleasant and something to be proud of, a kind of fatigue, just like riding a famous horse for dozens of miles. If being bold doesn't mean being careless, Xiangzi was not careless when he ran boldly. If you don't run quickly, you will be sorry for others; if you run quickly and damage the car, you will be sorry for yourself. The car is his life, and he knows how to be careful. Putting caution and boldness together, he became more and more confident. He was convinced that both himself and the car were made of iron.

Therefore, he not only dared to run boldly, but also did not think much about when to get out of the car. He felt that pulling a cart to earn a living was the most courageous thing in the world; if he was willing to go out, no one could stop him. He didn't pay much attention to the rumors from outside. He didn't pay much attention to the fact that there were soldiers in Xiyuan again, that there was another war in Changxindian, that another case was being investigated outside Xizhimen, or that Qihua Gate had been closed for a long time. Naturally, all the shops on the street had opened their doors, and the road was full of armed police and security teams. He didn't want to deliberately make himself feel uncomfortable, so he hurriedly collected the car like others. However, he didn't believe the rumors. He knew how to be cautious, especially since the car was his own, but he was a country boy after all, unlike city dwellers who heard wind and rain. Besides, his body makes him believe that even if he unfortunately gets to the "point", he will definitely have a way to avoid suffering a big loss; he is not easily bullied, with such a big stature and such broad shoulders!

News and rumors of war grow with the spring wheat almost every year. Wheat ears and bayonets can be regarded as symbols of the hopes and fears of northerners. When Xiangzi's new car was just half a year old, it was the time when wheat needed spring rain. The spring rain may not fall in accordance with the people's expectations, but war will always come regardless of whether anyone hopes for it or not. Rumor or truth, Xiangzi seemed to have forgotten that he had ever worked as a farmer; he didn't care much about how the war destroyed the fields, nor did he pay much attention to the presence or absence of spring rain. He only cares about his car, which can produce pancakes and all kinds of food. It is a versatile field that follows him docilely, a living land and a treasure land. Because of the lack of rain and the news of the war, the price of food has increased; Xiangzi knows this. But like the people in the city, he only complains about the price of food, but has no idea at all; food is expensive, yes, it is expensive, but who can make it cheap? This attitude made him only care about his own life and put all disasters and disasters behind him.

If the people in the city are helpless about everything, they may make up rumors—sometimes completely out of thin air, sometimes making up the truth—in order to show that they are not stupid and inactive. They are like small fishes. When they are idle, they put their mouths on the water surface and spit out a few completely useless bubbles. Among the rumors, the most interesting ones are about war. Other kinds of rumors are often just rumors, just like talking about ghosts and foxes, and they won't actually see ghosts after talking about them. Regarding the war, precisely because there is no correct information at all, rumors can have immediate effect. The short program may be very different from the real story, but as for the existence of the war itself, it is nine times out of ten correct. "There's going to be a war!" Once these words are uttered, there will definitely be a war sooner or later. As for who will fight whom and how, each person will have his own say. It’s not that Xiangzi didn’t know this. However, although the people who work hard - including cart pullers - will not welcome war, it is not necessarily bad luck if they encounter it. Whenever a war breaks out, the wealthy people are the most panicked. As soon as they heard the bad news, they quickly wanted to run for their lives; money made them come and run faster. They themselves can't run because their legs and feet are too heavy with money. They have to hire many people to make their legs, the boxes have to be carried, and the old and young men and women have to have carts to pull them. At this time, the hands and feet of the guys who specialize in limbs all become expensive: "Qianmen, East Station!" "Where?" "East-Train-Station!" "Oh, just give me one dollar and forty cents! No need to reject it, the war will be chaotic!"

It was under this situation that Xiangzi pulled the car out of the city. Rumors have been circulating for more than ten days, and the prices of things have increased, but the war seems to be still far away, and it will not reach Peiping for a while. Xiangzi continued to pull the cart as usual and was not lazy because of the rumors. One day, when he came to Xicheng, he saw some cracks. No one at the west entrance of Huguosi Street or Xinjiekou said "Where's Xiyuan? Where's Tsinghua University?" He wandered around Xinjiekou for a while. I heard that the carts were no longer allowed to leave the city. They were catching carts outside Xizhimen, including large carts, small carts, mule carts, and foreign carts. He wanted to drink a cup of tea, so he drove south; the calmness at the entrance revealed the real danger. He was quite courageous, but he couldn't deliberately take a dead end. At this juncture, two cars came from the south, and they seemed to be students. The driver of the cart walked sideways[1]

Shout: "Is there anyone who went to Tsinghua University? Hi, Tsinghua University!"

No one commented on the cars at the entrance. Some looked at the two cars and smiled lightly, while others sat with small cigarette pouches in their mouths without even raising their heads. The two cars continued to shout: "Are they all mute? Tsinghua University!"

"Two dollars, I'll go!" A young bald dwarf agreed jokingly when others were silent. "Pull over here! Find another one!" The two cars stopped.

The young bald man was stunned for a while, as if he didn't know what to do. Others haven't moved yet. Xiangzi realized that it must be dangerous to go out of the city. Otherwise, why would no one snatch two yuan from Tsinghua University—usually only twenty or thirty cents? He didn't want to go either. But the bald young man seemed to have made up his mind. If someone wanted to go with him, he would risk it all. He took a look at Xiangzi and said, "Big man, how are you?"

The word "big man" made Xiangzi smile. This was a compliment. His mind opened up: With such praise, it seems that the short and bold bald man should be praised; besides, two yuan is two yuan, this is not something that happens every day. Danger? Is it just that coincidental? Besides, someone said two days ago that the Temple of Heaven was full of soldiers; what he saw with his own eyes was that there were not even a single soldier there. With this thought, he pulled the car over.

When we arrived at Xizhimen, there were almost no pedestrians in the cave. Xiangzi's heart became colder. The bald man also saw that something was wrong, but he still smiled and said: "Hello[2]

, man! Is it a blessing or a curse? I am what I am today[3]

La! "Xiangzi knew that things were going to be bad, but after living on the streets for several years, he couldn't let it go and act like a bitch!

After leaving Xizhimen, he didn't even meet a car; Xiangzi lowered his head, not daring to look to the left or right of the road. His heart seemed to go straight to his side. When he arrived at Gaoliang Bridge, he took a look around and found no soldiers, so he felt relieved again. Two dollars is just two dollars, he thought, how could he find such a cool thing if he didn't have the guts. He usually didn't like to talk, but this time he was willing to talk to the bald dwarf. The street was so eerily quiet. "Shall we take the dirt road? On the road -" "Of course," the short man guessed what he meant, "as long as we get on the sidewalk, we will have some confidence!"

Before they could even get to the sidewalk, Xiangzi and the bald dwarf were captured by a dozen soldiers, including the car and the men!

Although it was already the time for temples to be opened in Miaofeng Mountain to offer incense, the coldness at night was not something that a single shirt could block. Xiangzi didn't have any encumbrances on him, except for a gray military uniform upper body and a pair of blue military trousers, both of which were stinky with sweat - they had been like this since they were on him. From this shabby military uniform, he remembered the white cloth jacket and the indane blue trousers jacket he originally wore; how clean and decent they were! Yes, there are many more decent things in the world than Danshi Linlan, but Xiangzi knows how difficult it is for him to get so clean and tidy. Smelling the smell of sweat on his body now, he regarded his previous struggles and successes as extremely glorious, magnifying them ten times compared to their original glory. The more he thought about the past, the more he hated those soldiers. They took away his clothes, shoes, hats, foreign car, and even the cloth belt around his waist. They only left him with bruises all over his body and blisters on his feet! However, clothes are nothing; the injuries on the body will heal soon. His car, the car he earned with years of hard work, is gone! It has disappeared since it was brought to the camp! All the previous hardships and difficulties can be forgotten in a blink of an eye, but he can't forget this car!

He is not afraid of hardship; but getting another car is not something that can be done casually; it will take at least several years of work! All the success in the past was in vain. He had to start a new business again! Xiangzi shed tears! Not only did he hate those soldiers, but he also hated everything in the world. Why do you bully people to this extent? Why? "Why?" he shouted. This cry--albeit a little more cheerful--immediately reminded him of danger. Don't worry about anything else, it's important to escape!

Where is he? He himself couldn't answer it correctly. These days, as he ran with the soldiers, sweat flowed from his head to his heels. To walk, he had to carry, pull or push the soldiers' things; to stop, he had to carry water, light a fire, and feed the animals. He only knew how to put the last strength into his hands and feet all day long, and his heart became blank. At night, he seemed dead the moment he hit the ground, and never opening his eyes again wasn't necessarily a bad thing.

At first, he seemed to remember that the soldiers retreated to the Miaofeng Mountain area. When he reached the back mountain, he only focused on climbing the mountain. He always thought that he might fall into the mountain stream and have his bones and flesh eaten away by wild eagles, without caring about anything else. After wandering around the mountains for many days, one day suddenly there were fewer and fewer mountain roads. When the sun was behind him, he saw the plains in the distance. The sound of the dinner bugle called back the soldiers who had left the camp, and some men with guns brought some camels.

camel! Xiangzi's heart skipped a beat, and suddenly he began to think, just like a lost person who suddenly found a familiar sign and remembered everything very quickly. A camel can't cross mountains, it must have reached flat ground. In his knowledge, he knew that camels were raised in three stores in the western part of Beijing, such as Balizhuang, Huangcun, Beixin'an, Moshikou, and Wulitun. Did he go around and around until he reached the entrance of the millstone? What kind of strategy is this - even if these soldiers who only know how to run away and rob have a strategy - he doesn't know. But he did know that if this was really Moshikou, the soldiers would not be able to get out of the mountain and would want to find a way to survive down the mountain. Moshikou is a good place. You can go back to Xishan to the northeast; you can go to Changxindian or Fengtai to the south; and you can go straight to the exit to the west. He planned this for the soldiers, and he also drew a way for himself: it was time for him to escape. If the soldiers retreated into the chaotic mountains again, he would be in danger of starving to death even if he escaped from the hands of the soldiers. If you want to escape, you have to take advantage of this opportunity. If he ran from here, he believed that he could run back to Haidian in one step! Although there were so many places in between, he knew them all; as soon as he closed his eyes, he had a map: This is the entrance to the millstone - God, this must be the entrance to the millstone! ——He turned northeast, passed Jinding Mountain, and the tomb of King Li, which was the Badachu; he ran eastward from the Fourth Platform to Xingzikou, and arrived at Nanxinzhuang. In order to get some shelter, he had better walk along the mountain, from Beixinzhuang to the north, passing Weijia Village; to the north, passing the South River Beach; and then to the north, to the top of Hongshan Mountain, to Prince Jie's Mansion and Jingyi Garden! After finding Jingyi Garden, he could touch Haidian with his eyes closed! His heart is going to beat out of his chest! These days, all his blood seemed to flow to his limbs; at this moment, it seemed that all his blood had returned to his heart. His heart was hot, but his limbs became cold; his whole body was trembling with desire!

Until midnight, he still couldn't close his eyes. Hope made him happy, fear frightened him, he wanted to sleep, but could not, and his limbs lay scattered on some hay. There was no sound, only the stars in the sky accompanied my heartbeat. The camel suddenly whined twice and was not far away from him. He liked the sound, which was as sad as hearing a cock crow suddenly at night, but also felt a little comforting.

There was the sound of cannons in the distance, very far away, but it was the sound of cannons clearly. He didn't dare to move, but chaos immediately broke out in the camp. He held his breath, the opportunity had come! He must have known that the soldiers had to retreat again, and they must go into the mountains. The experience of these days made him know that the fighting methods of these soldiers are the same as those of bees trapped in a house, they just bump around everywhere. With the sound of cannons, the soldiers must run; then, he himself should be more alert. He slowly, holding his breath, crawled on the ground, aiming to find the camels. He knew that the camels would not help him, but since he and they were both prisoners, it seemed that he must have some sympathy. The military camp became even more chaotic, and he found camels - lying like several mounds in the darkness. Apart from heavy breathing, there was no movement at all. It seemed that everything in the world was peaceful. This will teach him to be more courageous. He crouched beside the camel like a soldier behind a sandbag. He quickly came up with a theory: the sound of artillery came from the south. Even if it was not a true battle, it was at least a warning that "this road is blocked." Then, these soldiers have to flee back to the mountains. If they really went up the mountain, they couldn't take the camels with them. In this way, the fate of the camel is also his fate. If they had not given up the animals, he would have followed suit; if they had forgotten the camels, he could have escaped. He put his ear to the ground and listened for the sound of footsteps, his heart beating very fast.

I don’t know how long I waited, but no one came to pull the camel. He boldly sat up and looked between the camel's humps. He couldn't see anything. It was extremely dark everywhere. Run away! No matter good or bad, run away!

On one side, it's the same.

[2]

Say hello, do it, break in.

[3]

Today is today, which means that at a critical moment, success or failure will depend on today.

three

Xiangzi stopped just after running a few dozen steps. It turned out that Xiangzi was reluctant to part with those camels, because he only had one life now. Even if it is a piece of hemp rope on the ground, he is willing to pick it up. Even if it is useless, it can comfort him a little. At least he has a hemp rope in his hand and it is not completely empty. Escape is important, but what's the use of a naked life? He had to take away these animals. Although he hadn't thought of the use of the camels, they had to be something, and they were quite big.

He pulled the camel up. He didn't know much about how to treat camels, but he was not afraid of them because he came from the countryside and he dared to get close to the animals. The camels stood up very slowly, and he didn't care to check whether they were all tied together. When he felt that he could pull them away, he took a step forward, whether he was pulling up one of them or all of them.

As soon as he took a step, he regretted it. A camel—one accustomed to carrying weight in its mouth—cannot move fast. Not only do you have to walk slowly, but you also have to walk very carefully. Camels are afraid of slipping; a puddle of water or a patch of mud can cause them to split their legs or twist their knees. The value of a camel is all in its four legs; once the legs are gone, it's all gone! And Xiangzi wanted to escape!

However, he refused to put them down again. Everything is left to God. You can’t let go of a free camel!

Because he is used to pulling cars, Xiangzi has some ability to identify directions. Even so, he was a little confused now. When he found the camels, his heart seemed to be focused on them; when he pulled them up, he couldn't figure out which was which. The sky was so dark and his heart was so anxious. Even if he could look at the stars and adjust the direction, he didn't dare to do so calmly; the stars - in his eyes - seemed to be more anxious than him. You touch me, I touch you, moving around in the black sky. Xiangzi didn't dare to look at the sky anymore. He lowered his head, feeling anxious but not daring to move forward faster. He remembered this: since he was pulling a camel, he had to follow the road and no longer follow the hillside. It is a straight road from Moshikou - if this is Moshikou - to Huangcun. This is the camel's road, and it doesn't go far at all. "Don't go too far" has great value in the heart of a foreign rickshaw driver. However, there is no cover on this road! What if we encounter soldiers again? Even if he didn't meet any soldiers, would his shabby military uniform, mud on his face, and long hair make people believe that he was a camel puller? Not like a camel puller! Looks like a deserter! It is a trivial matter for a deserter to be taken away by the officials; if the people in the village catch him, he will at least be buried alive! Thinking of this, he started to tremble, and the thumping sound of camel hooves behind him suddenly startled him. If he wanted to escape, he still had to give up these burdens. But he refused to let go of the rope on the camel's nose. Let's go, let's go, wherever you go, say whatever you encounter; if you live, you can earn a few animals; if you die, accept your fate!

However, he took off his military uniform and tore off his collar; the pair of responsible copper buttons were also ripped off and thrown into the darkness without even making a sound. Then, he put the collarless and buttonless single coat diagonally across his body and tied the two sleeves into a knot on his chest, like a backpack. This, he thought, would reduce the suspicion of being defeated; he also rolled up his trousers. He knew it wasn't quite like a camel puller, but at least it wasn't quite like a deserter. Coupled with the mud on his face and the sweat on his body, it is probably enough to make him a "soot kid"[1]

. His thinking is very slow, but he thinks carefully and executes it as soon as he thinks of it. In the dark night, no one saw him; he didn't need to do this immediately, but he couldn't wait. He didn't know the time, maybe it would be dawn suddenly. Since he didn't follow the mountain road, he had no chance to hide during the day; if he wanted to keep going on the road during the day, he had to make people believe that he was a "coal black man." After thinking of this, he immediately did so. He felt a little happier, as if the danger was over and Peiping was in front of him. He had to get to the city steadily and quickly, because he didn't have any money or any solid food on him, so he couldn't waste any more time. Thinking of this, he wanted to ride a camel to save some energy and endure hunger for a while longer. But he didn't dare to ride it. Even if it was very stable, he had to teach the camel to kneel before he could get on; time was valuable, so he couldn't bother with it anymore. Besides, if he went up that high, it would be harder to see clearly what was under his feet. If the camel fell, he would have to accompany him. No, just leave.

He probably felt that he was walking along the main road; he was a little confused about the direction and location. It was late at night, and the fatigue of many days and the fear of running away made him feel physically and mentally uncomfortable. After walking some distance, his steps were so even and slow that he gradually seemed to feel sleepy. The night was still dark, and there was some cold and wet mist in the air, which made me feel even more confused. Looking hard at the ground, there always seemed to be bumps on the ground, but when I put my feet down, it was flat. This kind of caution and being deceived made him even less quiet, almost a little irritable. He simply ignored the ground, looked straight ahead, and walked with his feet rubbing the ground. Seeing nothing outside, it was as if all the darkness in the world were waiting for him. He stepped out of the darkness and then into the darkness; behind him was the silent camel.

As he gradually got used to the darkness outside, his heart seemed to stop moving, and he couldn't help but close his eyes. I don't know if I'm going forward or if I'm standing still. I just feel wave after wave in my heart, like a undulating black sea. The darkness is connected with my heart, all are vague, all ups and downs, all in a trance. Suddenly, something moved in my heart, as if I remembered something, and I seemed to hear some sounds, which I couldn't explain clearly; but I opened my eyes again. He was indeed walking forward, forgetting what he had just thought of, and there was no movement around. My heart beat for a while, then gradually calmed down again. He told himself not to close his eyes or think too much; getting to the city quickly was the first priority. But if he doesn't think about anything, his eyes will easily close again. He must be thinking about something and must be awake. He knew that if he collapsed, he could sleep for three days at a stretch. What are you thinking about? His head was a little dizzy, his body felt damp and sad, his hair was itchy, his feet were sore, and his mouth was dry and astringent. He couldn't think of anything else but pity himself. However, he couldn't even think about his own affairs in detail. His head was so empty and dizzy, as if he had forgotten himself as soon as he thought of himself, like a candle that was about to go out and couldn't even illuminate himself. Coupled with the darkness around him, he felt like he was floating in a cloud of black air. Although he knew that he still existed and was still moving forward, there was nothing else to prove where he was walking. It was like floating alone in the deserted sea and not trusting himself. He had never experienced such uncertainty and sadness, and absolute loneliness. Although he doesn't like to make friends on weekdays, he is not afraid of being alone in the sun, with the sun shining on his limbs and various things presented to him. Now, he wasn't afraid, but he couldn't bear the uncertainty of everything. If the camels were as dishonest as mules and horses, maybe he could be taught to cheer up and pay attention to them, but the camels were so docile, which made him impatient. When he was in the most dazed state, he suddenly doubted whether the camels were still behind him and startled him; he seemed to believe that these big animals would gently get into the dark fork in the road, but he had no idea that they would gradually melt away like pulling a block of ice.

At some point, he sat down. If he died like this, even if he was informed after death, he would not remember how and why he sat down. He sat there for five minutes, maybe an hour, he didn't know. He didn't know whether he sat down first and then fell asleep, or fell asleep first and then sat down. He probably fell asleep first and then sat down, because his fatigue made him fall asleep standing up.

He suddenly woke up. It was not the natural kind of waking up from sleep, but a sudden start, like jumping from one world to another, all in the blink of an eye. What he saw was still darkness, but he heard a rooster crow very clearly. It was so clear, as if something hard had scratched his brain. He was fully awake. What about camels? He couldn't think of anything else. The rope was still in his hand, and the camel was still beside him. His heart became quiet. Too lazy to get up. He was sore and lazy, and he didn't want to get up, but he didn't dare to sleep again. He had to think, think carefully, about good ideas. It was then that he remembered his car and shouted "Why?"

"Why?" But shouting in vain is of no use. He went to touch the camels, but he still didn't know how many he had pulled. I figured it out, there were three in total. He didn't think whether it was too much or too little; he focused his thoughts on these three animals. Although he had not yet decided what to do, he vaguely thought that his future depended on these three animals.

"Why don't you sell them and buy a car?" He almost jumped! But he didn't move, as if he felt ashamed for not having thought of such the most natural and easiest way before. The joy outweighed the shame, and he made up his mind: Didn't he hear the rooster crow just now? Even if the chickens sometimes crow at one or two o'clock at night, it is not far from dawn anyway. If there is a cockcrow, there must be a village, maybe Beixin'an? There was a camel breeder there, so he had to go quickly. If he could arrive at dawn and sell the camels, he could buy a car as soon as he entered the city. During the period of war and chaos, cars must have been cheaper; he only thought about buying cars, as if selling camels was an easy thing.

Thinking of the relationship between camels and rickshaws, his spirits became stronger, and there seemed to be nothing uncomfortable about him. If he thought that he could buy a hundred acres of land with these three camels, or exchange them for a few pearls, he would not be so happy. He stood up quickly, picked up the camel and left. He didn't know what the market for camels was now. He only heard that in the old days, when there were no trains, a camel was worth a fortune[2]

, because camels are stronger and eat less than mules and horses. He doesn't hope to get three big treasures, he just hopes to exchange them for a hundred and eighty yuan, just enough to buy a car. As he walked, the sky became brighter; yes, the light was in front, and he was indeed walking eastward. Even if he takes the wrong road, the direction is not bad; the mountains are in the west and the city is in the east, and he knows this. From uniform darkness everywhere, shades can gradually be distinguished. Although the colors cannot yet be distinguished, the trees in the fields and far away have taken shape in the general darkness. The stars were getting thinner, and the sky was covered with a layer of cloud-like and mist-like gray air. It was dim, but much higher than before. Xiangzi seemed to dare to raise his head. He also began to smell the grass on the roadside and heard a few birds chirping; because he saw vague objects, his ears, eyes, mouth and nose seemed to have resumed their normal functions. He could also see everything about himself, even though it was so tattered and messy, but he could believe that he was still alive; it was like waking up from a nightmare and feeling how lovely life was. After looking at himself, he looked back at the camel - just as ugly as he was, and just as cute. It is the time when animals shed their hair, and the gray-red skin of the camel has been exposed, with only some scattered, powerless long hair hanging here and there, which can be taken off at any time, like some huge beggars among beasts. The most pitiable thing is the long, hairless neck, so long, so bald, crooked, and stupid, stretching out far away, like a frustrated thin dragon. But Xiangzi never disliked them. No matter how disgraceful they were, they were still living things. He admits that he is the luckiest person in the world. God gave him three living treasures that are enough to exchange for a foreign car; this does not happen every day. He couldn't help but laugh.

There is some red in the gray sky, and the ground and distant trees are obviously darker; the red gradually blends with the gray, becoming gray-purple in some places, particularly red in some places, and most of the sky is grape gray. After staying for a while, bright golden yellow showed through the red, and various colors showed some light; suddenly, everything became very clear. Then, the morning glow in the east turned into deep red, and the sky above turned blue. The red clouds broke apart, and golden light shot out one after another. The horizontal ones were clouds and the straight ones were light, weaving into a spider web of great brilliance in the southeast corner of the sky: the green fields, trees, and weeds all changed from dark green to luminous emerald. The trunk of the old pine tree was dyed with golden red, the wings of the flying birds flashed with golden light, and everything brought a smile. Xiangzi wanted to shout a few times at the red light. Since he was pulled away by the soldiers, he seemed to have never seen the sun. He was always cursing in his heart, his head was always lowered, forgetting that there was still the sun, the moon, and God. Now, he was walking freely, and the sun became brighter and brighter as he walked. The sun gave a little golden light to the dewdrops on the grass blades, and also illuminated Xiangzi's eyebrows and hair, and warmed his heart. He forgot all hardships, all dangers, and all pains; no matter how dirty and dirty his body was, the light and heat of the sun did not exclude him. He lived in a universe with light and heat; he was happy, and he wanted to cheer!

Looking at the rags on his body and the three hairless camels behind him, he smiled. He thought, how strange it was that four such dishonorable men and animals could escape danger and walk towards the sun again! There is no need to think about who is right, everything is God's will, he thought. He felt relieved and walked slowly. As long as God protected him, he had nothing to fear. Where have you been? I don’t want to ask, although there are already men and women working in the fields. Let's go, even if he can't sell camels for a while, it doesn't seem to matter; he will go to the city first. He is eager to see the city again. Although there are no parents or relatives there and no property, it is his home after all, and the whole city is his home. Once he gets there, he will have something to do. There is a village in the distance, not a small village. The willow trees outside the village are like a row of tall and green guards. Looking down at the short houses, some smoke is floating on the houses. I heard the barking of village dogs in the distance, which was very pleasant. He kept running towards the village, not wanting to encounter anything funny, as if it was just to show that he was not afraid of anything, he was a good person, and of course he was not afraid of the good people in the village; now everyone is under the bright and peaceful sunshine. If possible, he would like some water to drink; even if he couldn't get water, it didn't matter; since he didn't die in the mountains, what was the point of being thirsty for a while?

The village dog barked at him, but he didn't pay much attention; the women and children looked at him, which made him feel uncomfortable. He must be a very strange camel puller, he thought: Otherwise, why are everyone staring at him blankly? He felt very embarrassed: the soldiers didn't treat him as a human being, and now when he came to the village, everyone looked at him like a monster! He didn't know what to do. His stature and strength have always made him proud, but in the past few days, he has suffered a lot of grievances and hardships for no reason. He looked over from the roof of a house and saw the bright sun again, but the sun didn't seem as cute as before!

On the only road in the village, pig urine, horse urine and sewage merged to form several smelly small lakes. In the north of Daoer, there was a relatively wealthy house with a tile-roofed house behind it. The gate was only blocked by a wooden fence, but there was no wooden door or gatehouse. Xiangzi's heart moved: A tile-roofed house means a rich man; a wooden gate without a gatehouse means a camel owner! Well, let him rest here for a while, in case there is a good opportunity to send the camel out!

"Se! Se! Se!" Xiangzi asked the camels to kneel down. Regarding the slogan for mobilizing the camels, he only knew that "Se..." meant kneeling down. He applied it very proudly and made a point of letting the villagers understand that he was not an amateur. The camels really knelt down, and he himself sat generously under a small willow tree. Everyone looked at him, and he looked at everyone; he knew that only in this way could the suspicion of the villagers be reduced.

After sitting for a while, an old man came out of the courtyard. His blue cloth coat was open and his face was bright. At a glance, he knew that he was a rich man from the countryside. Xiangzi made up his mind: "Old man, is the water ready? Have a drink!"

"Ah!" The old man rubbed the mud roll on his chest, looked at Xiangzi, and looked at the three camels carefully. "There is water! Where did it come from?"

"West!" Xiangzi did not dare to say the name of the place because he was not allowed to know.

"Are there soldiers in the west?" The old man stared at Xiangzi's military trousers.

"The soldiers wrapped him up and just escaped."

"Ah! There's no danger in camels going out of the west exit, right?"

"The soldiers are in the mountains and the road is peaceful."

"Yeah!" The old man nodded slowly. "Wait a minute, I'll get you some water."

Xiangzi followed in. When he arrived at the courtyard, he saw four camels. "Old man, can you keep three of my horses and join us together?"

"Humph! A handful? If we go back thirty years, I had three! Times have changed, who can still afford to feed the camels!" The old man stood still and looked at the four animals blankly. After staying for a long time: "A few days ago, I wanted to team up with the neighbors and take them outside the mouth to let them bloom.[3]

. There is a war in the east and a war in the west. Who dares to leave? Let's do Lasha at home, look at it and make you anxious, look at it and make you anxious, look at these flies! It's going to get really hot tomorrow, and mosquitoes will be added to the mix, and it'll be hard to see the good animals suffering. Alas! "The old man nodded repeatedly, seemingly filled with infinite emotions and complaints.

"Old man, leave three of my horses and put them together outside the mouth to let green. The animals that are jumping happily here will be killed by flies and mosquitoes all summer!" Xiangzi almost begged.

"But who has the money to buy them? This is no longer the time to raise camels!" "Keep them as much as you can; I'll sell them so I can make a living in the city!"

The old man looked at Xiangzi carefully and felt that he was definitely not a gangster. Then he looked back at the animals outside the door, and he seemed to really like the three camels. He knew that there would be no benefit in buying them, but book lovers wanted to buy them when they saw them, and horse breeders were reluctant to part with them after seeing them. The same goes for those who have owned three camels. Besides, Xiangzi said it can be sold at a low price; when a knowledgeable person gets a bargain, it is easy to forget whether there is any benefit in buying something.

"Young man, if I were rich, I would really want to stay!" The old man told the truth.

"Just stay and we'll see what happens!" Xiangzi was so sincere that the old man felt a little embarrassed.

"Seriously, young man, going back thirty years, this is worth three treasures; now, with the war and chaos, I - you should go somewhere else and shout!" "You will be given as much as you want!" Xiangzi couldn't think of anything else to say. He understood that the old man's words were true, but he was unwilling to sell camels all over the world - if they couldn't sell them, there might be something wrong with them.

"Look, look, it's hard to say twenty or thirty yuan, but it's really hard to get it out; these days, there's nothing you can do!"

Xiangzi felt a little cold in his heart, twenty or thirty yuan? We are still far away from buying a car! However, first of all, he wanted to get it done as soon as possible, and secondly, he didn't believe that he would meet another buyer by such a coincidence. "Old man, I will give you as much as you want!"

"What do you do, young man? It can be seen that you are not in this line of work!"

Xiangzi told the truth.

"Oh, you paid for these animals with your life!" The old man sympathized with Xiangzi and was relieved that they were not stolen. Although it was not far from stealing, there was still a layer of soldiers between them. After the war, nothing can be done according to common sense.

"Let's do it, man, I'll give you thirty-five dollars; I have to say it's not a bargain, I'm a coward; if I could get another dollar, I'd be a coward too! I'm over sixty. Huh, tell me what to say!"

Xiangzi ran out of ideas. When it comes to money, he has never been willing to relax. However, these days in the army, he suddenly heard the old man's sincere and emotional words, and he was embarrassed to argue anymore. Moreover, the thirty-five yuan available in cash seems more reliable than the hoped-for ten thousand yuan, although only thirty-five yuan for one life is indeed less! Just three big live camels, no, no, they are only worth thirty-five dollars! However, what can be done! "The camel is yours, old man! I just want one more thing, find me a little coat and some food!"

"That's okay!"

Xiangzi took a gulp of cold water, then took thirty-five pieces of very bright cash, two stick dough pancakes, and a shabby white coat that covered his chest, and was about to walk into the city!
