Learning resource sharing twenty-four

It's the time to burn incense to the top again, and the weather is getting hotter.

The paper fan sellers all seemed to suddenly appear from somewhere, carrying boxes with the bells on them ringing loudly and attracting attention. Beside the road, green apricots are being sold in piles, cherries are shining red, swarms of golden bees are settling on the pots of roses and dates, glass powder is placed in a large porcelain pot with a layer of opalescence, the picks of grilled cakes and jelly are arranged very neatly, and various colors of condiments are displayed. People also put on light and fancy single clothes. Suddenly, many colors are added to the street, like so many rainbows scattered in the world. The sweepers worked hard and kept pouring water on the road, but the light dust was still flying up, which was annoying. There are long willow branches and light and active swallows in the light dust, which makes people feel refreshed. It was an unbelievably good weather, and everyone was yawning lazily, tired and happy.

Yangko, Lion, Road Maker, Five Tiger Sticks, and other various groups all went up the mountain one after another. Beating gongs and drums, carrying boxes and cages, and flying apricot-yellow flags, one after another, gave the whole city some unusual excitement, gave people some vague but kind feelings, and left some sounds and dust in the air. Those who attended and watched the meeting felt some enthusiasm, piety, and excitement. The bustle of troubled times comes from superstition, and the only comfort for a fool is self-deception. These colors, these sounds, the clear clouds in the sky, and the dust on the street make people energetic and have things to do: those who go up the mountain, those who go to the temple, those who look at the flowers... Those who are not able to do it can still watch the excitement on the street and recite the Buddha's name twice.

The hot weather seems to have awakened the dream of spring in the old city. There are places to play everywhere and everyone thinks of something to do. The temperature encourages the flowers, plants, fruits and trees to grow together with the enjoyment of the world. The new green willows in the North and South Seas attract young people playing the harmonica. Men and women put the boats under the willow shades, or swing among the young lotuses, blowing love songs in their mouths, and kissing their eyebrows. The peonies and peonies in the park invite elegant people to wander slowly, waving precious paper fans; when they are tired of walking, they will drink a few cups of tea that can cause melancholy in front of the red wall, under the green pines, and secretly look at the ladies and famous flowers from the north and south coming and going. Even that place that has always been calm has been visited by gentle breezes and sunny days, bringing tourists as well as butterflies. The peonies in Chongxiao Temple, the green reeds in Taoranting, and the mulberry forests and rice in the Natural Museum all attract people's voices and shadows; even the Temple of Heaven, Confucius Temple, and Yonghe Temple are also slightly lively despite their seriousness. I like to travel far and wide with my students, to the Western Mountains, to hot springs, to the Summer Palace, to travel, to run around, to collect, and to scribble on rocks. People who are suffering from poverty also have places to go. Huguo Temple, Longfu Temple, Baita Temple, Tutu Temple, and Flower Market are all more lively than in the past: all kinds of flowers and plants are brightly displayed on the roadside, and one or two copper plates can bring "beauty" to your home. On the bean juice stall, the pickles are as bright as a big flower, with burnt red peppers on the tips. The chicken is cheap, and the fried egg horns are so brown and tender that it makes you salivate. The overpass is even more popular, with new tea tents built next to each other, white tablecloths, and charming singing girls, facing the old pines on the wall of the Temple of Heaven. The sound of gongs and drums was extended to seven or eight hours. The dry weather made the gongs and drums especially crisp, which disturbed people's hearts. It is easy for prostitutes to dress up. A single piece of floral fabric can be displayed beautifully and the curves of the body are clearly exposed. People who like peace and quiet have places to go. In front of Jishui Beach, outside Wanshou Temple, in the kilns in the eastern suburbs, and on the Baishi Bridge in the western suburbs, you can fish. The young reeds will tremble slightly when the small fish hit them. After fishing, pig head meat, boiled tofu, white dry wine and salt water beans in the wild teahouse can also make people drunk and full; then, carrying the fishing rod and small fish, along the willow bank, stepping into the sunset, calmly enter the ancient city gate.

It’s fun everywhere, lively everywhere, and lively everywhere. The intense heat in early summer is like a talisman, making this old city full of magic. It doesn't care about death, disaster, or hardship. When the time comes, it will exert its power and hypnotize millions of people's hearts, singing its hymns like a dream. It is dirty, it is beautiful, it is old, it is lively, it is messy, it is leisurely, it is lovely, it is the great Beiping in early summer.

It is during this season that people look forward to some news that is enough to relieve their boredom, news that is enough to read two or three times without getting bored, news that is enough to read the newspaper and see it in person. The days are so long and sunny!

Such news is coming! As soon as the tram came out of the factory, the newspaper seller was already running around shouting at the top of his voice: "News about the shooting of Ruan Minh, news about the parade at nine o'clock!" One copper plate, another copper plate, and another copper plate were all picked up by the little gangster. On the tram, in the shops, and in the hands of passers-by, one by one they all talk about Nguyen Minh: Nguyen Minh’s photos, Nguyen Minh’s history, Nguyen Minh’s interview notes, large and small characters, illustrations, the whole page is about Nguyen Minh. Nguyen Minh was on the tram. In the eyes of passers-by and in the mouths of those who talked to him, it seemed that there was no one else but Nguyen Minh in the old city. Nguyen Minh was paraded in the street today and was shot today! Valuable news, ideal news, not only talking about Ruan Ming, but you can also see him after a while. Women are rushing to dress up; old people go out early, fearing that they will be slow and fall behind; even children in school want to skip school for a long time and see something new. By eight-thirty, the streets were full of people, excited, hopeful, crowded, noisy, waiting to see this live news. The coachmen forgot to organize business, the rules in the shops were chaotic, and the vendors were too lazy to shout, and they were all looking forward to the prison car and Ruan Ming. In history, there were Huang Chao, Zhang Xianzhong, and the Taiping Heavenly Kingdom, who knew how to kill and loved to watch people kill. Shooting seems too simple. They like to listen to Ling Chi, beheading, skinning, and burying alive. It sounds like they have eaten ice cream, and they tremble slightly with pleasure. But this time, in addition to being shot, there was also a parade around the streets. They almost wanted to thank the person who came up with such an idea, so that they would see a half-dead man tied to the car, which made their eyes bright; even if they were not executioners, it was almost the same. These people have no good or evil in their hearts, do not understand good and evil, and cannot distinguish right from wrong. They cling to some ethics and want to be called civilized people; but they love to see their own kind chopped up with thousands of knives, as cruel and happy as a child slaughtering a puppy. Once they gain power, any one of them will massacre the city and cut off women's breasts and feet and pile them into hills. This is their quick move. If they don't get this authority, they might as well watch more slaughtering of pigs, sheep, and people to enjoy themselves. If they can't even touch this, they will curse even a child to kill with a thousand cuts, kill with ten thousand cuts, to relieve the evil in their hearts.

The clear blue sky, a red sun high in the east, a few light east winds, and the willow branches on the roadside swayed slightly. There is a large shadow on the east sidewalk, crowded with people: young and old, men and women, ugly, handsome, fat and thin, some dressed up nicely, some wearing only small coats, all talking and laughing, looking forward, and looking south or north from time to time. When one person poked his head in, everyone followed, and their hearts beat faster together. In this way, the crowd moved forward more and more, and the crowd gradually squeezed to the side of the road, becoming a wall of flesh, with only uneven heads moving around. Patrol police came out in groups to maintain order. They blocked and shouted. Sometimes they would pick out a kid who looked like a lump of clay and punch him in the face, which made everyone laugh. Waiting, waiting patiently, my legs are already sore, but I still refuse to go back empty-handed; those in front refuse to leave, and the newcomers behind them push forward, and they start to argue. They don't move their hands and feet, and they only use their words to fight, scolding each other, and everyone shouts "Hello". The child got impatient and was slapped by the adults; the pickpockets got hold of them and they were yelled at when they lost something. There was a lot of noise, shouting, and noisy, but no one was willing to move. The more people there were, the more reluctant they were to move, expressing their unanimous liking to look at the half-dead prisoner.

Suddenly, everyone was quiet, and a group of armed policemen came from a distance. "Coming!" someone shouted. Immediately after the crowd became noisy, the whole group of people crowded forward like a machine, inch by inch, and inch by inch, here they come! Coming! The eyes were all shining, something was being said on the mouth, there was a sound of people, the whole street smelled of sweat, the people of the country of ethics and ethics passionately loved to watch killings.

Ruan Ming is a short man, sitting in the car with his hands tied upside down, like a sick little monkey; his head is lowered, and a two-foot-long white stick is inserted behind his back. The human voice was like a tidal wave urging the back wave. Everyone curled their lips and criticized, and they were all a little disappointed: He is such a little monkey! It’s so fluffy and boring! He lowered his head, his face was pale, and he just remained silent! Some people thought of the idea and wanted to tease him: "Brother, call him a good boy!" Immediately afterwards, everyone from all directions shouted "good!" as if cheering for Kun Ling on the stage, shouting contemptuously, maliciously, and annoyingly. Ruan Ming still said nothing and didn't even raise his head. Some people were really anxious and looked down on such a soft prisoner. They crowded to the side of the road and spat at him. Ruan Ming still didn't move and showed no sign. The more everyone watched, the more bored they became, and the more reluctant they were to leave; what if he suddenly said: "In twenty years, he will be a good man again"? What if he wants to ask the hotel for two pots of dried meat and a plate of soy sauce? No one wants to move, let's see what happens to him. The car passed by and I had to follow him. He didn't show much performance now. How could he know that he would catch his breath and sing a few lines of "Shiro Visits His Mother" when he got to Danpailou? Follow! Some followed him all the way to the overpass; although he never did anything admirable or satisfying, people watched him take bullets, which was a worthwhile trip after all.

In such a lively season, Xiangzi walked slowly at the root of Deshengmen City alone with his head lowered. When he reached Jishuitan, he looked around. There was no one around, so he slowly and quietly walked up to the lake. I walked to the lake, found an old tree, leaned against the trunk, and stood for a while. Hearing that there was no sound around, he sat down gently. The reed leaves moved slightly, or a bird suddenly called out, causing him to stand up quickly, with sweat on his head. He listened and looked, but there was no movement around. He slowly sat down again. After doing this several times, he became accustomed to seeing the slight movement of the reed leaves and hearing the chirping of birds, and decided not to panic anymore. I stared blankly at some small fish in the ditch outside the lake, with eyes as bright as beads, gathering and scattering, coming and going; sometimes there was a piece of tender duckweed on their heads, and sometimes some foam was spitting out of their mouths. Beside the ditch, some tadpoles that have grown legs stand upright and swing their big black heads. The water suddenly flowed faster, washing away the small fish and tadpoles. Their tails were crooked and they went down the river. However, another group came along with the water, struggling to stop. A water scorpion ran over very quickly. The water flow gradually stabilized, and the small fish formed a team again, opening their small mouths to nibble on a floating green leaf or a piece of grass. The slightly larger fish hide in the depths, and occasionally reveal their backs, then turn around and go down, leaving whirlpools and some cracks on the water surface. The kingfisher grazed across the water like an arrow, and all the small and big fish disappeared, leaving only duckweed on the water. Xiangzi looked at these blankly, seeming to see it, but not seeming to see it. He accidentally picked up a small stone and threw it into the water. It splashed some water and scattered many duckweeds. He was startled and wanted to stand up again in fright.

After sitting for a long time, he secretly touched his waist with his big black hand. He nodded and kept his hand there; after a while, he took out a wad of banknotes in his hand, counted them, and hid them back in his place with great caution.

His heart was entirely occupied with that little money: how to spend it, how not to teach others, how to enjoy it and be safe. He no longer thinks for himself, he has become an appendage of money, and everything must be controlled by it.

The origin of this little money has already determined its path. This kind of money cannot be spent openly. Neither this little money nor the people holding it dare to see the sun. People were watching Ruan Ming on the street. Xiangzi hid in the quiet city root, trying to get to a quieter and darker place. He didn't dare to walk in the market anymore because he sold Ruan Ming. Even when he was alone facing the quiet water, with his back against the deserted city roots, he did not dare to raise his head, as if a ghost was always following him. Ruan Ming, who fell in blood on the overpass, is alive in Xiangzi's heart and in some banknotes around his waist. He didn't regret it, he was just afraid of the ghost that followed him everywhere and all the time.

After Ruan Ming became an official, he enjoyed some things that he had previously thought should be defeated. Money will lead people into a bad society, put aside their noble ideals, and willingly go to hell. He put on gorgeous foreign clothes, went whoring, gambling, and even smoked opium. When his conscience discovered it, he thought it was the evil society that framed him, and it was not entirely his fault; he admitted that his behavior was wrong, but the temptation to blame society was too strong for him to resist. Over and over again, he ran out of money, and he thought of those intense thoughts again, but he was not motivated to execute them; he wanted to use his thoughts in exchange for some money. Turning thoughts into money is like trying to get a passing grade by interacting with the teacher while studying. A lazy person's thoughts cannot coexist with his personality. Everything that can be exchanged for money will be sold sooner or later. He received a stipend. Organs that are eager to promote the revolution cannot choose their soldiers very carefully. Those who are willing to surrender are comrades. However, those who receive subsidies must have some achievements, no matter what means are used; what the agency wants is a report. Ruan Ming can't just take the money and do nothing. He participated in the work of organizing foreign rickshaw drivers. Xiangzi was already an expert in flag-waving and shouting. Therefore, Ruan Ming got to know Xiangzi.

Ruan Ming sells his ideas for money; Xiangzi accepts his ideas for money. Ruan Ming knew that Xiangzi could be sacrificed when necessary. Xiangzi had never made such a plan, but he did it when the time came - betrayed Ruan Ming. Those who work for money are afraid of encountering more money; loyalty is not based on money. Ruan Ming believed in his thoughts and used the intensity of his thoughts to forgive all his bad behaviors. Xiangzi heard what Ruan Ming said was very reasonable, but seeing Ruan Ming's enjoyment was also very enviable - "If I had more money, I would be happy for a few days! Just like the person named Ruan!" Money reduced Ruan Ming's personality, and money dazzled Xiangzi's eyes. He sold Ruan Ming for sixty yuan. What Ruan Ming wants is the power of the masses, and Xiangzi wants more enjoyment like Ruan Ming. Ruan Ming's blood was sprinkled on the allowance, and Xiangzi stuffed the banknotes into his waist.

He sat there until the sun turned to the west and the pampas grass and willow trees on the lake were covered with golden and red light. Only then did Xiangzi stand up and walk westward along the roots of the city. He is used to cheating money; this is the first time he sells people's lives. What's more, what he heard from Ruan Ming was very reasonable! The emptiness of the city's roots and the height of the city walls made him more and more afraid as he walked. Occasionally he saw a few crows on the garbage heap, and he wanted to walk away, fearing that they would startle them and give him some ominous crows. When he reached Xichenggen, he stepped up his pace and slipped out of Xizhimen like a dog that had stolen something. It was an ideal place to go at night to have someone with him, to numb him and make him unafraid; the white house was such an ideal place.

In autumn, Xiangzi's illness no longer allowed him to pull a cart, and Xiangzi's credit was so damaged that he could no longer rent a cart. He became the caretaker of a small shop. At night, with two copper plates, you can lie down in the shop. During the day, he went to do some work that could only earn him a bowl of porridge. He couldn't beg on the street. No one would be kind to him for such a big man. He doesn't know how to put some paint on his body and go to temple fairs to beg for money, because he hasn't been taught how to disguise the sores on his body into touching misfortune. He doesn't have the skills to be a thief. Thieves also have groups and connections. Only he himself will earn a living for himself, without any other dependence or assistance. He worked hard for himself and died for himself. He is waiting to take his last breath. He is a dead ghost who still has breath. Individualism is his soul. This soul will rot in the soil along with his body.

Since Peiping was designated as the old capital, its pomp, crafts, food, language, patrols... have slowly spread to the outside world, looking for places with the same majesty and financial resources as the emperor to support. The Westernized Qingdao also has Beiping's mutton-shabu-shabu; in the lively Tianjin, you can also hear the sad "hard noodles-饽饽" in the middle of the night; in Shanghai, Hankou, and Nanjing, there are also tourists who speak Beijing dialect. The police and officers ate sesame-sauce pancakes; the Xiangpian tea party went from south to north, passing through Shuangxun in Peiping and then south; even the pole bearers sometimes took the train to Tianjin or Nanjing to carry the coffins of high-ranking officials.

Peiping itself is gradually losing its original ostentation. You can still buy flower cakes after September 9th in the dim sum shops, and those who sell yuanxiao may have gone out of the market in the autumn. Those two or three hundred-year-old shops suddenly thought of commemorating the anniversary, so as to distribute leaflets about big sales... Economic oppression has forced the ostentation to find another way, and dignity cannot be used as food. However, weddings and funerals generally retain the old rituals and style. Weddings and funerals seem to be noteworthy but more or less pompous. The butlers, ringers, wedding sedans and official covers for weddings and funerals are not something any city can match. The pines, cranes and lions used for funerals, the paper-made sedans and horses, the full set of butlers used for weddings, and the twenty-four rattles still appear in the market in an official style, reminding people of the prosperity and magnanimity of the peaceful era.

Xiangzi's life mostly relies on these remaining rituals and rules. For those who were getting married, he held flags and umbrellas for them; for those who were undergoing funerals, he held wreaths and elegiac couplets for them; he was neither happy nor crying, he only accompanied them on the streets for the ten coppers. Putting on the green or blue robe prepared by the gang house or the wedding sedan shop, and the inappropriate black hat, he could temporarily cover his rags and look a little more decent. When he met a wealthy family who was doing business and asked everyone to shave their heads and wear boots, he had the opportunity to make his head and feet neat and tidy. His heart disease prevented him from moving forward, so he just walked slowly along the roadside holding a flag or two elegiac couplets.

However, he is not even a good person at doing this. His golden age has passed. He has never started a family or established a career in a foreign car. Everything has become "that way" according to his wishes. He is so big, but he is always trying to fly a flying tiger flag, or a pair of short and narrow elegiac couplets; he refuses to move the heavier red umbrellas, silence cards, etc. He will also compete with an old man, a child, or even a woman. He refused to suffer any loss.

Holding such a small thing, he lowered his head, bent his back, holding a cigarette end picked up from the road in his mouth, and slowly and feebly rubbed it. When everyone is standing still, he may still leave; after everyone has left, he may stand for a while; he seems not to hear the sound of the gong giving orders. He never looked at the evenness of the distance before and after, or whether the queues on the left or right were neat or uneven. He walked in his own way, with his head lowered as if he was dreaming, or as if he was thinking about some profound truth. The gong man in red and the deacon holding the silk flag almost cursed him in all the village dialects: "Grandson! I'm talking about you, Camel! You're fucking on par!" He didn't seem to hear it yet. The gong player went over and gave him a gong hammer. He rolled his eyes and looked around dimly. Regardless of what the gong player said, he looked carefully on the ground to see if there were any cigarette butts worth picking up. Decent, strong, dreamy, self-interested, personal, strong, great, Xiangzi, I don’t know how many funerals he has accompanied others; I don’t know when and where he will bury himself, this depraved, selfish, unfortunate, born in the womb of social disease, the doomed ghost of individualism!
